Longtime West Virginia Sen. Rockefeller dies

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Former Sen. Jay Rockefeller, a five-term West Virginia Democrat and champion for the rural poor, has died at the age of 89.

Rockefeller, who retired after the 2014 elections, played key roles on the tax-writing Senate Finance Committee, where he helped write landmark children’s health insurance legislation, as well as the Commerce, Science and Transportation and Intelligence panels which he chaired.

A staunchly progressive lawmaker, he represented a deeply conservative state that turned further to the right in his later years. Rockefeller, scion of a famous New York family renowned for its wealth, never wavered in his convictions and retired rather than face a potential backlash from voters upset by the then-Obama administration’s attempts to rein in the coal industry.

Sen. Shelley Moore Capito, R-W.Va., who challenged Rockefeller for his seat before he decided not to seek reelection, released a statement Friday praising her predecessor’s legacy.

“Jay dedicated his life to serving West Virginia and our nation, leaving a lasting legacy. He was always a true gentleman who treated me with respect, kindness, and encouragement,” Capito said. “Over the years, we developed a wonderful friendship that transcended politics, and I will always be grateful for that.”

West Virginia’s Republican Gov. Patrick Morrisey — himself a transplanted Northerner, with roots in Brooklyn and New Jersey — lauded Rockefeller’s record on health care and aid for coal miners and veterans in their adopted state.

“His work helped shape our state and left a lasting mark on the lives of generations of West Virginians,” Morrisey said.

Rockefeller was born in Manhattan, NY on June 18, 1937, the great-grandson of Standard Oil Co. founder John D. Rockefeller, who was once considered the world’s richest man. Rockefeller attended Harvard University, graduating with an A.B. in 1961, but his passion was for the far-east and spent three years studying at the International Christian University in Tokyo.

He returned to the states and was appointed by President John F. Kennedy to the National Advisory Council of the Peace Corps in 1961, spearheaded the Peace Corps initial program in the Philippines. He worked for a brief time in what was then known as U.S. Department of Far Eastern Affairs before deciding to embark on a public service role of a more personal nature. He enrolling as a VISTA volunteer, in the program which would later be known as Americorps, and moved to the small mining community of Emmons, West Virginia in 1964 at age 27.

In West Virginia, Rockefeller found his life’s work. He described the experience as being “reborn,” and spoke often the poverty and hardship he encountered living in the small town. “I could not leave,” he said of West Virginia. “I [had] become so devoted to those people and the unfairness.

He had planned to stay only one year, but decided to stay and became active in local politics. He broke from his family’s long tradition of Republican loyalty and ran successfully for the House of Delegates as a Democrat in 1966. Two years later, he was elected Secretary of State.

Rockefeller lost a bid for governor in 1972, but he became the president of West Virginia Wesleyan College and continued to place roots in the state. He married Sharon Percy Rockefeller in 1967 and they started their family. When he ran for governor again in 1976, he won, and served two terms before winning election to the U.S. Senate in 1984, ultimately serving five terms before retiring in 2014.

Rockefeller was determined to increase economic opportunities for West Virginia, for whom he felt was forgotten and abandoned by the rest of the country. He called the people of his state “ the hardest working people I know,” and believed that bringing more business opportunities would help lift the state out of severe rural poverty and the dependence on the coal industry.

Rockefeller traveled to Japan and worked tirelessly to bring a Toyota manufacturing plant to Buffalo, W.Va, one of over twenty Japanese companies he’d brought to West Virginia. He fought for fair trade policies and targeted tax relief to protect West Virginia industries such as steel and chemicals.

Coal mining had long been the economic backbone of West Virginia, but Rockefeller felt more must be done to protect miners, many of whom worked in unsafe conditions or fell ill with Pneumoconiosis, or Black Lung Disease, after years of inhaling coal dust.

But defying the coal industry came with a political cost. When Rockefeller originally ran for governor in 1972, he had opposed mountaintop mining, also called strip mining, or the practice of removing large amounts of earth and rock in order to access underground seams of coal. When he ran again in 1976, he reversed his position and won.

But even as he ascended to the Senate, Rockefeller remained both friend and foe to the coal industry.

Despite growing up on the Upper East Side of Manhattan, figuratively and literally hundreds of miles away from coal mines, Rockefeller understood its deep roots in West Virginia’s economy and culture. He believed it was the miners — rather than industry — for whom he was defending.

He hung a century-old photo of a coal miner in his Senate office in Hart, and would point to the photo and remark to visitors that “this is why I’m here and who I work for.”

Rockefeller fought to secure pensions for miners, especially as coal companies would file for bankruptcy and immediately restructure to do away with pension plans.

In 1990, Rockefeller was one of only 11 senators to vote against the bipartisan Clean Air Act, which he felt disadvantaged workers in West Virginia. But those close to him said the decision weighed heavily on him, and Rockefeller privately worried that the lack of such regulations would pollute the air and destroy waterways, the brunt of which would be born by the same disadvantaged people whom he vowed to protect.

He believed strongly in the deliberative aspects of the legislative process, consuming great amounts of reading material to familiarize himself with the issues. During a time of increasing partisan rancor, Rockefeller still reached across the aisle to find a willing partner, even when there was not one to be found.

In 2010, as the Environmental Protection Agency’s greenhouse gas regulations were coming to fruition, Rockefeller came up with a compromise to delay implementation for two years.

He viewed himself as a broker between industry interests and the hard working people from coal-states like West Virginia. He had hoped that his years of standing up for coal miners and West Virginia interests would foster enough goodwill among industries primed to oppose any compromise that was less than an all-out rejection of the existing EPA statutes.

But industry walked away from Rockefeller, preferring a hard-line opposition to EPA regulations, and lobbied other senators to vote against Rockefeller’s measure. Furious, Rockefeller took to the floor of the U.S. Senate to denounce the coal industry and its leaders, an unprecedented action for a popular coal-state senator.

“The reality is that many who run the coal industry today would rather attack false enemies and deny real problems then find solutions,” he said on the Senate floor on June 19, 2013 . “Scare tactics are a cynical waste of time, money and, worst of all, coal miners’ hopes. But sadly, these coal operators have closed themselves off from any other opposing voices and few dared to speak out for change — even though it’s been staring them in the face for years.”

His fury against the coal industry was heard loudly, many speculating that he would retire rather than face re-election.

He had won re-election to the Senate handedly during his five campaigns. In 2008, his final election, he was re-elected with nearly 64 percent of the vote. But six months following his fated coal speech, in January 2013, Rockefeller announced he would retire at the end of his fifth term.

Rockefeller’s legacy may best be known for his fight to expand health care for vulnerable populations, including children, the economically disadvantaged and the elderly. Rockefeller served as one of the chief congressional champions for President Bill Clinton’s proposed health care overhaul, later he played a critical role and was a loud progressive voice in writing and passing the Affordable Care Act in the Senate.

Rockefeller believed strongly in a public health insurance option, offering it as an amendment in the Senate Finance Committee during the ACA markup. A public-run insurance plan, he said, would put patients before profits, and help those people, especially West Virginians, with high healthcare costs and poor coverage who were left “out in the cold.”

“These are people,” Rockefeller said, visibly emotional during his closing arguments to the Senate Finance Committee. “Eleven-year-old kids. These are families, and we have to respect them. And you respect them by giving them a choice.”

The amendment failed. Rockefeller pivoted to strengthen Medicaid and Children’s Health Insurance Program provisions, believing that poor and disadvantaged families could seek refuge within those programs. CHIP had been one of Rockefeller’s signature legislative victories, helping to write that program initially in 1997 and later working on a major expansion that became law in 2009.

Despite his initial concerns and later disappointments — at one time saying publicly he could not support ACA as it stood — he later brought his support, citing it as a win for all Americans and actively lobbied his colleagues to support it.

Rockefeller was also known for his role on the Senate Intelligence Committee. A former chairman, he was critical of the nation’s mismanagement of the Iraq war and clashed frequently with Republicans over the treatment of detainees. After he retired from Congress, Rockefeller served as a distinguished fellow at the Council on Foreign Relations.

Respect for Rockefeller ran deep on Capitol Hill. He was beloved by staff and known as a thoughtful, deliberative lawmaker. He had a fondness for classical music and served as a board member of the Bach Consort, an organization in Washington devoted to the study and performance of the music of Bach and his contemporaries. He admired art and Asian culture, and served as a board member of the Asia Society. He was known as a man dedicated to his work and his adopted state of West Virginia.

Rockefeller is survived by his wife, Sharon Percy Rockefeller, four children: John (Emily) Rockefeller V; Valerie (Steven) Wayne; Charles Rockefeller; and Justin (Indre) Rockefeller; and nine grandchildren.

Disclosure: the author served as a staffer in Rockefeller’s Senate office from 2009-2011.

Équipe de France : Zaïre-Emery ou Akliouche, le dilemme de Zidane !

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Zinédine Zidane annonce officiellement sa deuxième liste à la tête de l'équipe de France ce vendredi 6 novembre 2026 à 18h00 depuis le siège de la FFF. Dans un entrejeu tricolore contraint de se réorganiser, un duel direct oppose Warren Zaïre-Emery et Maghnes Akliouche pour intégrer le groupe.

À quelques heures de l'annonce officielle, l'incertitude demeure quant aux choix définitifs du sélectionneur pour composer son milieu de terrain. Selon RMC Sport, Warren Zaïre-Emery et Maghnes Akliouche sont en balance directe pour décrocher une place dans l'entrejeu de l'équipe de France. Zinédine Zidane doit en effet opérer des ajustements dans ce secteur à l'occasion de sa deuxième liste à la tête des Bleus. Un casse-tête. Alors que Rayan Cherki avait été installé dans un rôle de milieu relayeur lors du premier rassemblement de septembre, le sélectionneur national hésite désormais entre ces deux joueurs pour compléter sa ligne médiane, sans qu'aucun ne soit encore assuré d'être retenu.

Cette redistribution des cartes au milieu de terrain découle principalement de mouvements forcés au sein de l'effectif, Manu Koné étant d'ores et déjà forfait. Toutefois, Zinédine Zidane enregistre une nouvelle rassurante avec le retour sur les terrains d'Aurélien Tchouaméni, qui devrait réintégrer le groupe tricolore. Par ailleurs, Valentin Atangana (Al Ahli) a reçu une pré-convocation après avoir impressionné le staff avec trois passes décisives lors d'un match supervisé avec les Espoirs. Un secteur en pleine recomposition. D'autres postes subissent également des absences sur blessure avec les forfaits d'Ousmane Dembélé, Esteban Lepaul, Jules Koundé, William Saliba et Maxence Lacroix.

Le verdict sera officiellement rendu ce vendredi 6 novembre 2026 à 18h00, heure à laquelle Zinédine Zidane annoncera sa liste de joueurs depuis le siège de la Fédération Française de Football (FFF). Ce rassemblement concerne les deux dernières rencontres de la phase de poules de la Ligue des nations. L'équipe de France occupe la tête du groupe 1 de la Ligue A avec 10 points en quatre matches, devant l'Italie (7 points), la Belgique (6 points) et la Turquie (0 point). Un match nul lors de l'une des deux rencontres suffit aux Bleus pour valider leur billet pour les quarts de finale. L'Italie accueillera la France le jeudi 12 novembre à Milan (20h45 sur TF1), avant la réception de la Turquie le dimanche 15 novembre à Bordeaux (20h45 sur TF1).

Mercato - Ça bouge à l’OM : Qui doit être le prochain joueur à partir cet été ?

Bayern Munich: Vincent Kompany banned from talking football with his assistants

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Bayern Munich do not want to take any risks with the health of Vincent Kompany. Forced to stay at home to recover from meningitis, the Belgian coach was given particularly strict instructions by his management: a total ban on talking football, even with his own assistants. Weakened by illness after having suffered several illnesses in the exercise of his duties, the 40-year-old technician must observe a period of complete rest. Present at a press conference on Friday, on the eve of the Bundesliga clash against Borussia Dortmund, his interim replacement Aaron Danks confirmed this decision. “ No, absolutely not. We take the situation very seriously. I received clear instructions from Max Eberl and Christoph Freund: not to initiate such discussions and ensure that Vince only focuses on his health and spending time with his family “, explained the Englishman, when asked about possible tactical exchanges with Kompany before the Klassiker.

The Bavarian leaders thus wish to completely cut their coach off from the daily life of the club, in order to allow him to regain all his abilities before considering a return to the bench. A decision which concerns both the preparation of meetings as well as the tactical choices or the composition of the teams. “ We want him to rest and recover properly so that when he returns he is in great shape and can help us as best as possible. We are very strict and we completely exclude football from this situation “, insisted Aaron Danks. Released from hospital after his meningitis, Vincent Kompany should remain away from his duties at least until the end of the next international break, scheduled for November 9 to 17. In the meantime, his deputy will have to manage the first team, particularly during the game against Borussia Dortmund. Bayern is therefore favoring a complete recovery for its coach, without rushing his return despite the important sporting events awaiting the club.

After taking over EA, Saudi Arabia and their private equity cronies have reportedly held talks about buying Unity

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The engine builders have, it's claimed, been working with an investment ​bank to evaluate [their] options

Members of the consortium led by the Saudi Arabian government's Public Investment Fund and some private equity suits which took over EA this year in a leveraged buyout worth $55 billion reportedly have a new target in their sights. Unity, software company and makers of the game engine bearing the same name, have held talks with the consortium over a potential takeover, it's claimed.

According to Reuters, a consortium including Saudi Arabia’s sovereign wealth fund and private equity firm Silver Lake Partners recently approached Unity about a deal that would value the company at around $25 billion. Unity are, it's claimed, currently working with an investment bank to evaluate their options following the chat, with no deal set in stone right now.

Silver Lake, Reuters note, are already among Unity's largest shareholders and invested up to $400 million in the engine builders in 2017. In fact, Silver Lake's managing director and CEO Egon Durban already has a seat on Unity's board of directors, so will probably have to butt out of any takeover talks.

Assuming the consortium would go the leveraged buyout route, loading Unity up with debt like the EA takeover did, it's hard to see any deal not resulting in similarly catastrophic consequences for those working at Unity. EA laid off a reported 400 people in an effort to cut costs after coming under the boot of the Saudi state and private equity, shutting BioWare and cancelling something Sims-related codenamed Project Lotus. BioWare's demise has since been reported to have largely come down to EA execs not wanting to fund both Star Wars Battlefront 3 and Mass Effect 5, with them opting to back only Battlefront 3.

Unity's recent financial results are looking strong, propped up by year-on-year growth in an AI-powered ad network dubbed Vector, so one would assume that bit of the business likely wouldn't be top of the list if any bloodletting was to end up happening.

Solidarity to any Unity workers who're now having to live under the shadow of this prospective takeover and the upheaval it could lead to if it goes ahead in a fashion which mirrors the EA deal.

Democratic sweep gives markets a reason to rethink path of interest rates

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Democrats' resounding victory in the U.S. midterms has the potential to curb interest rate hikes globally next year, according to market watchers — but the uncertain path of oil prices, U.S. economic growth and fiscal policies will be key.

Central banks around the world are back in monetary tightening mode, with the Federal Reserve, Bank of England, European Central Bank and Bank of Japan all raising rates in recent months.

The shift has in part stemmed from the U.S. and Israel's war with Iran driving up prices in oil and gas markets. The war has also exacerbated a squeeze in global refining capacity which has sent the cost of products like diesel, gasoline and jet fuel soaring.

Traders largely expected big Democratic gains this week after polls pointed in their favor, but there was still uncertainty leading up to the vote about whether the party would capture both chambers of Congress, increasing the ability for lawmakers to challenge President Donald Trump's policymaking.

Crude oil prices tumbled following the clear-cut Democratic victory in both the House and Senate, with international benchmark ICE Brent crude falling 8.7% on Wednesday, closing below $100 a barrel for the first time since September.

That was largely attributed to raised expectations that the result may mean Trump is more inclined to strike a deal to end the war in Iran, helping restore Gulf refinery capacity and improve export conditions. Vice President JD Vance's lengthy statement blaming Republican losses on the Iran war boosted that view.

Dan Coatsworth, head of markets at AJ Bell, said there had been "an admission from the Republican camp that the Iran war cost them a clean sweep in the election, which the market has taken to indicate that a swift resolution to the Middle East conflict could help them save face."

"Trump might relish the opportunity to take all the credit if an end to the war leads to a big drop in energy costs."

If the election result "pushes for a resolution of the conflict in the Middle East, that might bring oil down, and that's been an issue for the Fed and inflation, and part of the reason why rates are higher," said Andrew Slimmon, senior portfolio manager and head of the Applied Equity Advisors at Morgan Stanley.

Historic data shows a tight correlation between oil prices and global rates, Ralph Axel, rates strategist at Bank of America, said in a research note published Thursday. Higher oil prices over the next three to six months would likely mean "significantly higher interest rates" in the U.S., but especially so in the U.K. and Germany, he said.

"If oil prices rise, we think rates will too, especially at the front end where long-end term premium is less of a factor and monetary policy expectations play a larger role," he added.

As the uptick in crude prices on Thursday and choppiness on Friday showed, a resolution to the nearly nine-month-long conflict is far from locked in.

"For now, oil has begun to climb back up again, and interest rate expectations have not radically changed from pre-election levels," AJ Bell's Dan Coatsworth noted.

Money markets on Thursday indicated a moderate change in expectations from a week ago, both in the U.S. and Europe, with traders pricing in a slightly higher chance of monetary easing on a one-year horizon.

The effect on a two-year timeframe was more pronounced in the euro area than the U.S., according to CNBC analysis of LSEG data, with rate pricing down 8.7 basis points on the week for the ECB versus 4.6 basis points for the Fed.

"Republicans don't really have an off-ramp here at this stage," said Laurel Rapp, director for the U.S. and North America Program at Chatham House. "We know the Democrats will seek to take votes in the House and the Senate to constrain the Republicans, to restrain the administration on Iran war powers. But whether that will bind the administration is another matter."

Eurasia Group's Clayton Allen meanwhile told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" that while the war with Iran is unpopular at home, leaving the Middle East region without tangible wins would also be unpopular and that he expected an escalation within weeks.

Andrew Bailey, governor of the Bank of England — which hiked rates by 25 basis points on Thursday — told CNBC that energy price pressures stemmed from a combination of obstructions on the Strait of Hormuz, a shortage in refining capacity, and low European gas storage.

"A peace process in the Middle East will not shift all of those elements," he told CNBC's Steve Sedgwick on Thursday.

"Let's wait and see, because we have seen that fall in energy prices over the last 48 hours, but it is a very volatile situation, as we've learnt over the last seven, eight, nine months."

Ken Egan, head of European Sovereign Credit at KBRA, said the interest rate outlook would be primarily impacted by whether economies have enough underlying momentum for existing inflation pressures to become more persistent.

Europe is "heading into winter after months of elevated energy prices, and the full impact is still feeding through to the wider economy," he said by email.

"The concern for central banks is whether these pressures start to push up wages and prices more broadly, or whether weaker demand limits that pass-through."

A key question for the Federal Reserve will be whether the blue sweep "could lead to more fiscal prudence in the U.S. and thus less inflation," Jane Foley, head of FX strategy at Rabobank, said by email.

"It is very early, however, to be sure of the fiscal outlook, not least because many Democrats were voted in to soften cost of living pressures ... so it is very difficult to come to a firm conclusion at this point as to how the outlook for rates will change next year," she said.

The high level of U.S. fiscal stimulus and whether it is about to change is the "million dollar question" right now, James Sullivan, managing director and head of Asia Pacific equity research at J.P. Morgan, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia."

"The reasons why you're seeing inflation running high is a combination of a range of factors, from the war in Iran, to tariffs, to significant tightness in particular areas of the energy complex," he said.

"But it's also important to recognize the U.S. government has continued to spend at very high levels, into an economic expansion with low unemployment, and that's driving very specific macroeconomic implications. If that changes, you potentially take the pressure off the rate environment in a way that gives the Fed significantly more forward flexibility."

Daniel Clifton, head of policy research at Baird Strategas, said the impact on the data center buildout in the U.S. was another big factor to watch.

"In 2025, we had major fiscal headwinds, and it really didn't matter because we had such a big capex boom in the AI build-out last year. This year was driven a lot by stimulus," he told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe."

"If you have an environment where you have tighter fiscal policy — the Fed is raising rates right now — and you have a slowdown in the AI capex build out, you're probably going to reach a policy mistake at some point in mid-2027," he said.

"That's what happened in 2000 during the Y2K tech boom, the Fed was raising rates, we had higher oil prices, and they just went too far, and eventually it had a recession. Our forecast is not for a recession in the United States. Our forecast is that we'll have a mid-cycle slowdown in 2027."

Cole Palmer has been left out of Thomas Tuchel’s England squad for the final two Nations League fixtures of 2026, despite being available for selection.

The Chelsea playmaker had been expected to come back into contention after withdrawing from the previous international camp with a fitness issue.

Instead, Tuchel has chosen to move forward without him for the games against Croatia at Wembley and Spain in Madrid, adding another twist to Palmer’s complicated year with the national team.

According to The Athletic, Palmer has not been selected despite being fit enough to feature for Chelsea.

The 24-year-old pulled out of England’s September and October camp after suffering a minor muscular problem, but his latest omission is understood to be a selection decision rather than an injury-enforced absence.

That makes the decision particularly notable given Palmer’s recent club form. Sky Sports reported that he had contributed three goals and an assist since returning to action, including an impressive Champions League goal in Prague.

Palmer had been recalled by Tuchel in September after missing out on England’s World Cup squad, only to withdraw shortly afterwards.

This feels like a significant moment rather than a routine squad omission.

Tuchel has already made it clear that Palmer has missed too many opportunities to work with him at international level.

CaughtOffside reported in September that the England manager described Palmer’s withdrawal as another “missed opportunity” and stressed that players need to be physically present in camp to properly impress him.

That context makes this latest call more understandable, even if it will frustrate Chelsea supporters.

Palmer’s talent is not really in question. His ability to create chances, score from midfield and decide tight games gives England something different. The issue now appears to be whether he can establish enough consistency and availability to convince Tuchel that he should be a regular part of the squad.

England face Croatia on 12 November before travelling to Spain on 15 November, with a place in the Nations League knockout rounds still at stake.

For Palmer, the message looks clear: strong Chelsea form alone may no longer be enough.

The New York Times Company is reportedly trying to buy Letterboxd for $330 million

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Letterboxd would reportedly continue operations as a standalone site.

The company that owns The New York Times is in "exclusive negotiations" to purchase the cinephile online platform Letterboxd, anonymous sources told... The New York Times. The price being discussed is reportedly around $330 million, significantly more than the $250 million figure that was bandied around earlier this year. Discussions are ongoing and could still break down, the report states.

Letterboxd would reportedly continue operations as a standalone site for the "foreseeable future," the NYT reported. However, the Times Company could introduce its own advertising technology into Letterboxd's systems. (The New York Times Company declined to confirm the New York Times' reporting.)

With its "social film discovery" tagline, Letterboxd allows members to create curated lists, diaries, logs, ratings and reviews of movies, while following other members' activities — a Goodreads for movies, if you will. Though relatively small with 30 million members, Letterboxd is an influential online film site with a dedicated and active community that includes notable figures like Martin Scorsese. The site is projected to have $15 million in revenue for 2026.

The Canadian investment group Tiny purchased 60 percent of Letterboxd in 2023, but reportedly started seeking a sale in July this year at a valuation of around $250 million. The list of potential suitors at the time was a who's who of Hollywood, with Netflix, Paramount and Sony Pictures all reportedly interested.

The New York Times Company is now in the mix at a higher price, and is arguably a better buyer because it wouldn't pose a conflict of interest like those Hollywood studios. The Times Company also has a similar business model with a mix of ad and subscription revenue.

It's only been two weeks since we last heard Warren Spector sound off about EA's sudden decision to close BioWare, following the publisher's acquisition by Saudi Arabia's PIF and venture capitalist concerns. But now, the storied developer is back and - apparently - angrier than ever.

"I recently posted my thoughts about EA’s shuttering of BioWare," Spector wrote in a post on LinkedIn. "Those of you who read my post know that I was unhappy. I remain so. The tragedy of so many layoffs the last few years hasn’t been lost on me, but for some reason, BioWare hit me on an emotional level that nothing before had. I felt gut-punched."

Spector goes on to counter the idea that the studio was closed down as a "sound business decision" because the "studio’s glory days (artistic and commercial) were behind them" and that "businesses don’t succeed on history alone". These reactions, more than anything else, appear to be what's inspired Spector to take to posting again - he simply cannot agree with this assessment.

"If studio-level people leave, resulting in dilution of talent, someone is creating an environment that pushes them out. Do those culture-killers pay any price? If executives misread the market, over-hire, or make decisions that result in under-funded, over-funded, poorly-marketed games do they lose sleep let alone jobs?" he asks.

"Do those same executives suffer like developers when the needs of financial partners and private investors take precedence over the people who actually create value, let alone over the interests of players? Does anyone up there in or adjacent to the C-suite care? Is there any accountability left in this world? Obviously not."

He then goes on to list the yearly payouts that some of the highest-level executives at EA are rewarded per annum. These include:

"Tens of thousands of do-ers and makers are out of work and these people think they're worth that kind of compensation?" continues Spector. "And if you think this is a discussion about 'sound business decisions', I hope you never get anywhere near running a business. Or at least that people have the sense not to go work for you."

Back in August, we saw Warren Spector announce his intention to retire from the world of video game development. Spector's announcement comes shortly after the last game he worked on - Thick as Thieves - had its final major update only a few months after its release. Despite its charm and low price, Thick as Thieves sadly didn't sell what it needed to, resulting in layoffs at Spector's studio.

Zcash's NU7 Upgrade Goes Live, Recycling 60% of Fees to Fund Future Block Rewards

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Zcash's NU7 Upgrade Goes Live, Recycling 60% of Fees to Fund Future Block Rewards

Zcash's NU7 network upgrade went live at block 3,543,405 early Friday, activating the Network Sustainability Mechanism (NSM), a change designed to help pay for the privacy coin's security as its block rewards shrink.

"NU7 has successfully activated on mainnet. A new era of Zcash is here," the project's official account tweeted.

NU7 has successfully activated on mainnet. A new era of Zcash is here. $ZEC pic.twitter.com/rPaSFe9h7j

"This is a huge milestone for Shielded Labs," Jason McGee of Shielded Labs, the Zcash development organization behind the mechanism, tweeted ahead of the activation.

The NSM tackles a problem Zcash inherited from Bitcoin: whether, as block subsidies decline with each halving, transaction fees alone will be enough to pay miners to secure the network. Under the mechanism, 60% of transaction fees will be removed from circulation and gradually reissued through future block rewards, while preserving the 21 million ZEC supply cap. Reissuance will begin in 2031, with the recycled funds spread along a smooth curve on top of the existing halving schedule.

The NSM was Shielded Labs' first project and builds on an idea that developer Nate Wilcox proposed years ago, originally called the Zcash Posterity Fund, McGee said. A separate proposal under consideration would require Zcash Community Grants to contribute 50,000 ZEC from its treasury to the NSM.

— Jason McGee 🛡 (@aquietinvestor) November 5, 2026

NU7 also brings other changes. It cuts Zcash's target time between blocks from 75 seconds to 25, the Zcash Foundation said, and retires Sprout, Zcash's original privacy pool from 2016, leaving coins worth about $16 million locked in the pool. Faster blocks raise the risk of orphan blocks, valid blocks that the network discards because another miner's block reached the rest of the network first. In a stress test of 80 nodes spread across 11 regions, with every block full of private transactions, developers of Zakura, which last month proposed a quantum privacy fix for shielded swaps, measured an orphan rate of 3.43% at 25-second blocks, compared with 1.57% at 75 seconds. They expect the live network's rate to stay under 1%, since real mining is far more concentrated and blocks far emptier.

NU7 also limits how much private transaction activity can fit into each block, reducing the data wallets must scan to find their payments, according to the upgrade specification.

Shielded Labs' original design would have replaced Zcash's halvings with a smooth issuance curve. Community panels supported that change, but coinholders did not, so an alternative design that kept the halvings was developed instead.

"We still think the original design was simpler and better, but we respect the process that led us here," McGee wrote. "It should be difficult to change the protocol, and the process should resist capture by any single organization, group, or influential individual."

The upgrade arrives as Zcash's community debates the future of Zcash Community Grants (ZCG) and its share of the Dev Fund, the slice of block rewards that pays for development.

The five organizations that coordinate Zcash's coinholder voting, Project Tachyon, Shielded Labs, Valar Group, the Zcash Foundation and ZODL, have set out an expedited timeline for proposals and polling, saying "tensions were high" and that a drawn-out debate risked "deepening divisions within the Zcash community."

Proposals are due by November 8, with polls for coinholders and community panels running from November 16 to November 30. If rough consensus emerges, the groups will support a further network upgrade, NU 7.1, to implement it.

Raul Jimenez reveals major four-word update that Wolves fans will love

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Raul Jimenez has shared a fresh update on his ongoing recovery from an MCL injury, which will provide a serious boost to Wolves fans as the Mexican striker's return to action slowly but surely edges closer.

The good times are rolling right now at Wolves, who have ascended to second in the Championship behind league leaders Middlesbrough ahead of Saturday lunchtime's crunch trip to Wrexham following three successive victories over Watford, Cardiff and Bristol City.

It has, of course, relieved some much-needed pressure and scrutiny upon the shoulders of Cesar Peixoto, though the Portuguese boss is still not completely out of the woods and remains searching for the backing of some of the Old Gold faithful since his arrival from Gil Vicente to replace Rob Edwards over the summer.

Nonetheless, Wolves are beginning to truly find their rhythm and that's only made all the more impressive by the continued absence of Jimenez, who begun the campaign in strong form upon his emotional homecoming to the Black Country but has not kicked a ball in anger since September.

Jimenez has, however, revealed a new progression update relating to his recovery update, and the words used will provide delight to supporters, teammates and Peixoto alike as all at Molineux eagerly anticipate the veteran frontman's return.

The 36-year-old has been out of action since before the three-week international back in September after suffering a serious MCL injury 36 minutes into Wolves' 1-0 EFL Cup third-round defeat at Everton, in which many believed he shouldn't have been risked in the first place.

Peixoto, then, was left to rue his selection decision when the 130-cap Mexican international's injury turned out to be more severe than expected, forcing the Championship promotion hopefuls to initiate ligament surgery instead.

Surgery would see Jimenez ruled out of proceedings until January, representing a significant blow for Wolves in the wake of a lightning-fast reintroduction to life at Molineux after lighting up the World Cup with co-hosts Mexico by scoring three goals from four matches upon leaving Premier League outfit Fulham.

Wolves' patchy form following the international window amid defeats to both Middlesbrough and Bolton Wanderers ensured that his absence was sorely-felt, although others have stepped up to the mantle in recent weeks to allow the ex-Benfica and Fulham striker to focus entirely on his recovery process instead of rushing to get back sooner rather than later.

The experienced striker recorded his 500th career appearance against Bristol City on Wednesday

His latest update is, however, an extremely positive one, and it may leave the Old Gold faithful optimistic of an even earlier return to the pitch.

Jimenez has kept supporters in the loop with many social media posts and updates throughout his time on the treatment table, which will only be appreciated by the club's fans as they await his return.

He's been stepping up his recovery process in recent weeks, moving on to more intensive physical work as his condition continues to improve.

Jimenez has generally appeared to be in good spirits, too, and that's evidenced by his latest update.

Speaking via his Instagram story on Thursday, Jimenez wrote: "Making really good process 🔥💪🐺."

Just four words were required for an admission that will leave Wolves supporters delighted, with the caption accompanying a video of the goal-getting ace undertaking leg exercises in a bid to regain his strength and mobility.

Type the top scorer next to each season. Surnames count. Two seasons had joint winners, so they need two names.

Top league scorers in the regular season, per season since the second tier became the Championship in 2004–05.

It was either side of a series of stories posted to show Jimenez going through his paces with some arduous gym work, and he's certainly not prepared to let up or allow this injury to get the better of him as he continues to work harder and harder ahead of a return early in the new year.

Jimenez's fellow frontmen at Wolves have begun to find the back of the net with increasing regularity in recent times, and he'll only be hoping that trend continues until he's back on the pitch himself.

One of the main worries when Jimenez suffered his injury was about exactly who was going to come to the fore and provide the cutting-edge in the final third to compensate for his long-term absence after kicking off his second stint at Wolves with three goals from six Championship fixtures.

Thankfully, both Adam Armstrong and Sean Neave are finding form to ensure that Jimenez's absence doesn't end up derailing Wolves' campaign.

Armstrong, who has largely struggled for form since arriving from Southampton in February, looks to have rediscovered his goalscoring boots - which are esteemed at this level, of course - after recording his first strikes since August in back-to-back wins against Watford and Cardiff.

He wasn't on the scoresheet during Wolves' most recent midweek victory at Bristol City, where Newcastle United loanee Neave instead stepped up to the billing by appearing from the bench to score his first professional goals in style with a late brace in the final 10 minutes to seal a dramatic 2-1 away victory for the white-hot visitors.

Wolves fans, how encouraged are you by Raul Jimenez's latest update? Are you excited for his return in the coming months? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

The pair must not let up, and will each have plenty of opportunities to continue stamping down their authority before Jimenez makes his way back, but Wolves' upturn in form alongside the attacker's striking counterparts providing the goalscoring goods and a key four-word recovery update from the man himself means that Wolves fans have much to feel positive about right now.

The Best Survival Food, Tested and Reviewed

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

We may earn revenue from the products available on this page and participate in affiliate programs. Learn more

Regarding survival, a reliable food supply is high on the list of needs for disaster readiness. A well-rounded, easy-to-transport kit that keeps you nourished for days—without repeating the same bland meals—is crucial for morale and sustenance.

Many survival food companies are on the market, but not all have stood the test of time. Over the years, several have failed due to poor quality control and mismanagement. As a disaster readiness and survival training professional, I receive samples from many of these companies and, more importantly, have the unique opportunity to have hundreds of students test their products yearly. Additionally, I bring these samples to our courses to gather feedback from our instructors and participants. The insights shared in this article are based on decades of personal experience and the collective input of our students, providing you with a well-rounded perspective to help you make informed decisions about your food preparedness.

At the end of the day, no matter the crisis, we all need food to keep us going. While it’s true that humans can survive for weeks without eating, very few would willingly test that theory. The good news is that with the growing demand, more high-quality, nutrient-dense options are now available. After testing some of the best survival food on the market, these are our top picks for flavor, shelf life, and nutrition.

4Patriots has established itself as one of the leading survival food brands in the market. Proudly made and packaged in the United States, their products support local businesses while delivering high-quality meals. These meals are crafted for superior nutrition and taste, using fresh ingredients and whole grains, and are free from MSG. Preparation is simple—boil water, add the pouch, and within 15 minutes, you’ll have a hot meal ready to eat.

4Patriots packages their food in triple-layer Mylar to ensure long shelf life, which helps protect the contents from air and moisture. Customers can select from various kit sizes depending on their needs, from 72-hour emergency supplies to year-long provisions. Each kit is designed to provide one person with six servings a day, offering sufficient calories and nutrients to sustain energy. The meals come in various flavors, including macaroni and cheese, vegetable soup, and oatmeal. One of my favorite pieces of their kits is that many of the meals are named after people to add some morale build into it all.  No matter the kit size, 4Patriots offers a reliable solution for building a robust survival food supply.

A vital feature of an MRE is the built-in heater, which, once activated, heats your entrée, providing the convenience of a hot meal no matter where you are. However, there are some notable downsides. Many of our students, particularly those with military experience who have relied on MREs for extended periods, advise against using them for long-term situations. In addition to being heavy to transport, they can lead to digestive problems, ranging from constipation to more organ-related severe issues over time.

Providing a hot meal for your family during a disaster is more than just a practical necessity—it can help boost morale and alleviate anxiety, offering a sense of normalcy amidst the chaos. Mountain House meals taste delicious and require boiling water for preparation. Mountain House is a regular staple of meal choices in our classes. I see them often.

One of the great features is that the food rehydrates directly in the pouch, allowing you to eat from it, which means minimal cleanup afterward. With nearly 30 meal options, including breakfasts and desserts, variety won’t be an issue, and no one will find the meals repetitive.

Each serving offers about 250 calories, depending on the meal. With two servings per pouch, there’s plenty for even a hearty appetite or enough to share if needed. These meals are designed to stay fresh for up to 30 years and ensure you can access quality food for decades.

Crafted with Whey Protein Isolate as the primary protein source and fortified with high fiber while containing just 1 gram of sugar, these protein bars are perfect for daily enjoyment. Each Kirkland Signature bar offers 21 grams of high-quality protein and features real chocolate for a satisfying blend of taste and nutrition. Typically used for meal replacement and workout-focused diets, these bars conveniently meet your protein and fiber needs without compromising flavor.

In the context of disaster preparedness, these protein bars are an ideal addition to any emergency food supply. Their long shelf life, combined with their nutritional profile, makes them an excellent option for staying energized and nourished when fresh food may not be available. Lightweight and easy to store, they offer a reliable source of protein and fiber that can help maintain energy levels and support overall health during a crisis. Whether sheltering in place or on the move, these bars ensure access to essential nutrients in challenging circumstances.

Augason Farms is renowned for its extensive line of canned goods, which are perfect for long-term food storage and emergency preparedness. Their #10 cans are filled with freeze-dried or dehydrated foods, offering various essentials, such as fruits, vegetables, meats, grains, and dairy products. With a shelf life of up to 25 years, these canned goods are an excellent choice for those looking to build a reliable food supply for emergencies or everyday convenience. Each can is sealed for freshness and labeled with detailed preparation instructions, making incorporating these products into daily meals or survival situations easy.

Augason Farms’ canned goods are appealing because of their versatility and nutritional value. Whether you need individual ingredients like powdered eggs, instant potatoes, or freeze-dried strawberries, their products allow for flexibility in meal planning. You can use them in recipes or enjoy them independently, ensuring you have access to nutritious food options when fresh ingredients are unavailable. These canned goods are compact, easy to store, and offer large quantities, making them ideal for families, survivalists, or anyone seeking to stock up on essential food supplies that can withstand the test of time.

Peak Refuel is a top choice for disaster preparedness, offering high-protein, freeze-dried meals designed to keep you fueled during challenging situations. Maintaining strength and energy is essential in emergencies, and Peak Refuel meals deliver an impressive 40-50 grams of protein per serving. This high protein content ensures you get the nutrition needed to stay alert and physically capable when it matters most. Whether you’re sheltering in place or need to be on the move, these meals provide the sustenance required to get through physically demanding conditions.

Beyond their nutritional value, Peak Refuel meals are incredibly convenient for disaster preparedness. Made with high-quality, non-GMO ingredients, they rehydrate quickly with hot water and are ready to eat in minutes. The lightweight, resealable pouches are easy to store and transport, making them an ideal addition to any emergency food supply. Various meal options, including breakfast scrambles and hearty dinners like chicken alfredo or beef stroganoff, offer the nutrients you need and great taste to keep morale high during tough times. Peak Refuel’s focus on protein-rich, easy-to-prepare meals makes them an excellent choice for anyone serious about disaster readiness.

ReadyWise is a leading provider of long-term food storage solutions, offering various affordable, high-quality meals that are perfect for disaster preparedness. Whether you’re planning for a short-term emergency or looking to build a comprehensive survival food stockpile, ReadyWise provides convenient, easy-to-prepare options. Their freeze-dried meals only require water, making them ideal for emergencies or outdoor adventures where resources may be limited. With a focus on both taste and nutrition, ReadyWise ensures that their products not only sustain you during a crisis but also offer flavors you can enjoy.

One of the standout features of ReadyWise is its budget-friendly approach, making it a top choice for those who want to prepare without breaking the bank. Despite the lower price point, ReadyWise doesn’t compromise on quality or variety, offering satisfying and nutritionally balanced meals. This combination of affordability and convenience makes ReadyWise a smart investment for families and individuals looking to secure a reliable food supply for potential emergencies. With ReadyWise, you can build your preparedness plan without overspending, ensuring peace of mind for whatever the future holds.

To select the foods we tested, we received samples from a variety of reputable companies and also purchased additional products to ensure a wide range of options. With the help of our students, who have diverse tastes and preferences, we got a broad spectrum of feedback during the testing process. These foods were integrated directly into our survival and disaster readiness training courses, allowing us to evaluate their practicality, taste, and ease of preparation in real-world scenarios. This hands-on approach ensured that we tested the food’s quality and performance under survival conditions.

A structured approach to survival prioritization is best understood through the “Law of Threes” hierarchy. This principle outlines that our most immediate needs, in order of importance, are safety, thermoregulation, hydration, and finally, food. However, from a psychological and moral standpoint, food remains a constant desire, even though it ranks lower on essential needs.

We also took a multi-tiered approach to assessing these survival food options.  I am fond of saying that “hunger is the best seasoning in the world.”  That is true in the long term, but taste plays a role in the short term.  When assessing them, I emphasize both taste and cost-effectiveness. After all, it makes little sense to invest in provisions that are not enjoyable to eat. In addition to these factors, I consider the following critical criteria in my recommendations:

One of the most overlooked factors when choosing survival food is whether or not you enjoy eating it. Taste, texture, and how the food agrees with your digestive system are essential. While it’s easy to focus on stockpiling a large quantity of food, if you don’t like it, you’re essentially wasting money. Some may argue that you’ll eat anything in extreme hunger, but right now, you have the luxury of selecting food you’ll enjoy. Regularly incorporating these meals into your current diet helps rotate stock and prepares your taste buds for when disaster strikes. As the saying goes, “We do not rise to the occasion; we default to our level of training.” Why not start training your palate now?

Cost is another critical consideration when building a survival food supply. Most people must budget carefully, so simultaneously making large purchases may be impossible. A gradual approach works well for many, where small amounts are bought over time as the budget allows. This makes the process more manageable and helps you steadily grow your stockpile without straining your finances. If you face resistance from family members about spending on survival food, consider framing it as an insurance policy—something you invest in for peace of mind in case of a worst-case scenario.

Storage space can be a real challenge for those living in smaller spaces like apartments, condos, or tiny homes. It’s crucial to make the most of your space by considering the size of the packaging and the number of servings per product. This ensures you get the most food stored in the least space. Carefully evaluating your storage setup and choosing compact, efficient products can help maximize your shelf space and investment.

Storing survival food is best done in conditions that are dark, cool, and dry. These three factors will work together to slow down the degradation of the food. A closet in a spare bedroom, for example, might be a great location. A damp basement, perhaps not so much.

Most survival situations tend to last around 72 hours. However, in recent years, many emergencies have extended beyond that timeframe, and with global uncertainty, the possibility of more prolonged events is not just possible but increasingly likely. As a precaution, I recommend planning for at least one month’s worth of food for everyone in your household, ensuring you’re prepared for longer-term scenarios.

As previously mentioned, our primary needs follow a specific order: safety, thermoregulation, water, and food. For safety, ensure you have the tools necessary to protect yourself from both environmental threats and potential harm from others. This includes items to help you see in low-light situations and defend yourself if needed. Thermoregulation involves staying warm in cold weather and cool in hot conditions, so be sure to have adequate clothing and supplies to maintain comfort for at least one month without access to utilities.Water is an invaluable resource, and it’s crucial to have both filtration and purification methods to protect against bacterial and viral contaminants. In addition to these essentials, it’s wise to have signaling tools like bright-colored items, flashlights, and whistles to help attract attention if necessary.

Choosing survival food to keep on hand for emergencies requires a little homework and research. You shouldn’t buy several cases of something simply because the package looks enticing. Base your decision on facts, including budget and the quality of the food. Ease of preparation is also a factor, of course. In an emergency, you may not have ready access to things like stovetops or microwave ovens. Stick with things that require a minimal amount of preparation. Hopefully, you’ll never be in a situation where you must rely on survival food, but you’ll be glad you have it should the unthinkable occur.

Our writers and editors eat, sleep, and breathe the outdoors, and that passion comes through in our product reviews. You can count on F&S to keep you up to date on the best new gear. And when we write about a product—whether it’s a bass lure or a backpack—we cover the good and the bad, so you know exactly what to expect before you decide to make a purchase.

Port Vale confirm TV decision and one thing is causing a real backlash

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Port Vale supporters have learned the new Sky Sports TV schedule just after the new year, and it would be to suggest that it has not gone down particularly well amongst the fanbase.

Port Vale, after a tricky start back to life in League Two following relegation from League One last year, are starting to finally starting to find their footing, as Jon Brady's side aim to claw their way back up the table.

There remains hope amongst the Valiants that this year could have a dream conclusion, though for those that often travel away with the club, they have just received some bad news.

Which sets of EFL fans have the most misery induced by their clubs?

Port Vale has confirmed via X that some fixtures around January and February 2027 have been moved to fit in with Sky Sports' TV coverage, with one of those being the away trip to Exeter City.

The initial kick-off time at St James' Park was a standard 3 pm on Saturday, February 20, but now that has been brought forward by two-and-a-half hours to a 12:30 pm kick-off time, giving Vale supporters an incredibly early morning to look forward to.

From Vale Park in Stoke-on-Trent, it's a near four-hour trip down to Devon without any stops and perfect traffic, as now supporters will have no choice but to get an even earlier night to be wide awake for the trip down south.

Naturally, this news hasn't gone down well with Vale fans, who have taken to X to react to the update.

An eight-hour round journey, at best, is already difficult enough as it is, but make that an early kick-off, and it will no doubt prevent a lot of supporters from attending who otherwise would've.

The changing of schedules is completely out of any supporters' hands, and Vale have been shown absolutely no consideration or mercy in this instance.

'Joke' is extremely apt to describe this situation.

This change in kick-off time can be enough for anyone to fall out of love with football, especially if you're on the receiving end of it.

You'd do well to find a supporter, Vale or not, who would agree that this fixture change is a good idea, as once again, the supporters are not considered in this instance.

What do you make of the fixture change? Have Sky considered the Vale supporters in this? How would you feel if your team was on the receiving end? Share your thoughts below!

Whilst Vale supporters don't have to worry about the trip down to Devon for another few months, in the meantime, head coach Brady may well have helped his side turn a corner.

The Valiants won just one game in their opening nine fixtures this season, the sole victory incidentally coming against Exeter, but with five wins and one defeat in the following seven, Vale finally look to have found their feet.

By the time they travel to Exeter, supporters hope to be looking up instead of over their shoulder, as an immediate return to League One shouldn't yet be ruled out.

Scalpers Already Want Insane Sums For The Ocarina Remake With Exclusive Bits

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

There's nothing new with secondhand sellers trying their luck to make a big profit from day one. It doesn't help when special editions like the Fire Emblem: Dagdan Collection sell out almost instantly, either. The FOMO is real and scalpers gonna scalp.

However, it seems that sellers on eBay UK are taking things to fresh extremes with the Switch 2 Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake, with some Buy it Now listings for the foil cover version plus a couple of exclusive extras listed for over £700.

As you'll know if you read Jim's excellent feature on the London midnight launch, 500 copies of the game were available to buy on Wednesday night, and Nintendo threw in a cardboard replica of the original PAL N64 box and a little Ocarina keyring for anyone willing to queue on Tottenham Court Road on a chilly autumn night.

And as you'd expect, some copies from this midnight event soon found their way onto eBay. But even knowing how these things go, the asking prices are shocking.

Feature "I Will Have Been Here For 30 Hours" - London's Midnight Zelda Launch Was Wild

£646.77. £700.17. £513.91. £717.25. £696.62... and it goes on. If you're happy to sacrifice the Ocarina keyring, one seller in France has the game and the replica box for a thoroughly reasonable £321.62 + £54.22 postage. Another has just the box and the keychain — not even the game — listed for £479.89 or Best Offer. Hmm, might a tenner get a bite?

For context, the sold-out Zelda-themed Switch 2 console (which is £434.99 retail price) is listed for less than some of these scandalous Buy-it-Now punts.

Obviously, these sellers are hoping to snare a Jabu-Jabu who simply cannot live without a slightly disappointing facsimile of an old N64 box. Likewise, the foil cover version of the remake is notionally "limited", but we've yet to see the standard variant amongst NL staff's copies, and some of us just wandered into a supermarket on launch day.

Similar box-based bonuses were available at retailers in other territories, so this familiar pattern of speculative auction listings will crop up everywhere.

As always, if you're desperate to own any of these cute bonuses, you do you. Unsightly corrugated pattern aside, the box is a neat little nod to the original game, and who doesn't like a cute keyring? But having procured a couple of these "RARE" boxes, we can confirm they are almost certainly not worth 700 of your quids.

Consider this: You could get a PAL copy of the original game in decent condition, plus the remake and any number of alternative Ocarina keyrings, for far, far less.

Let us know below if you managed to get these bonuses, and how much you'd be prepared to pay for a small cardboard box.

I worked a season at GameStop in 2007 when I was between jobs, and even by then I had people regularly in there looking for flipping opportunities, like some woman calling her boyfriend while browsing "babe this Halo 3 helmet thing says limited edition what do you think we can get for it?" Back in the 90s it was the short packed action figures, people lining up at Toys R Us to get Thomas Riker or black outfit Storm or whatever.

As always, people wouldn't be devoting their time standing in line for this stuff with the intent to flip if it wasn't a way to make some scratch on the side. If people had a modicum of restraint, flippers wouldn't be so prevalent with stuff like this. But someone will drop three figures on a cardboard box, probably chasing the dopamine hit of showing it off on reddit.

Just grabbed a couple items on eBay myself! I stupidly threw away my own without thinking. So, I managed to find the game box cellophane wrapper, the insert card that highlighted all the Zelda titles available with an NSO subscription, and a vial containing that "new game" smell. And all of this for a steal at $250!

I was quite happy with my preorder bonuses. I ordered through Rakuten Books in Japan and got a ocarina cleaning cloth, an art card set with a stand and the best item was a wooden statue of link with a wooden map of Hyrule as a separate background piece. Almost good enough to be a special edition.

Please, people, don't buy from them. I didn't even have to try to get a foil cover for GameStop, and my friend got three foil ones (one for herself and picked up two as gifts for other friends lol) at a random Walmart

Maybe I'm doing this "collector" thing wrong, but none of this is worth those insane prices. Especially the N64 box which, while cool, doesn't even hold anything? To me that's just a step away from buying air, like in The Lorax.

Pro tip: It’s only a scalp if you pay what they ask

Why bother playing the game when you can stare at the sparkly box for hours.

I don't know why Nintendo UK decided not to make this a standard pre-order bonus and allowed for this sort of thing to happen.

Pokemon already showed Nintendo how to deal with scalpers, mass production, make so many they become basically worthless on the secondary market. "Oh a 30th anniversary Pikachu card, how quint I threw out 12 of them last week, can't seem to get rid of them, they're littering the streets."

Just buy it off Etsy if you want an empty box. This is just a repro box the front of the box and the back is exactly the same.

What do you mean by already?It's been this way for months!

I can't imagine that many people expected anything less than this predicament.

This is going to sound crazy, especially coming from me, but I at least understand why these scalpers are charging what they do.

I stopped selling on fleabay and mercari because of the ridiculously high seller fees!

I used to sell movies online and in person, but used to sell $5 for the movie. It got to where I had to start selling for $15 or more just to make that $5!

Between fees, taxes, shipping material, postage, tracking number and signature delivery, I just couldn't do it anymore.

Then you also have people that say they never got it or it is broken or that it is the wrong one so I'd be out both money and product!

I know at the the end of the day money is fiat (at least in the us) and thus is essentially monopoly money but do people actually pay these prices? Even wealthy folk?

I spent a lot on the zelda launch…but it was retail price.

Square Enix responds after Nier Automata update triggers massive 366GB storage requirement

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Nier Automata players have been left confused after a new update increased the game’s file size from 50GB to a whopping 366GB, forcing Square Enix to apologize and investigate the issue.

Games have been demanding more and more storage on both PC and console, with new releases like Modern Warfare 4 and Phantom Blade averaging well over 100GB each. Cleaning up space on your SSD has become a routine occurrence for anyone looking to play the latest releases.

However, Nier Automata fans on Steam planning to slash their way through Yoko Taro’s post-apocalyptic adventure were met with an eye-watering storage requirement when Steam demanded 366GB of free space to install the 50GB game.

The added bloat follows the November 6 update, which added Simplified Chinese, Traditional Chinese, and Korean language support. Since then, players have reported that this has caused Steam to incorrectly count the full game’s size for each of the game’s DLCs.

In a post on the official Japanese Nier Automata account, Square Enix apologized to players affected by the issue.

was thinking of replaying nier automata but i forgot square enix is run by terrorists pic.twitter.com/S9DicWvopB

“We have confirmed an issue where, after applying the latest update to the Steam version of Nier, the installation process displays a storage requirement higher than expected,” the translated statement reads.

“We are currently investigating the cause and working to resolve the issue. We apologize for the inconvenience.”

For anyone encountering the problem, Square Enix recommends opening Nier Automata’s Properties menu in Steam, selecting DLC, and unchecking the listed content. This temporary workaround allows players to download the base game without having to deal with the inflated storage requirement.

« C’est une soirée parfaite » : Auteur d'un joli but, Amine Gouiri libère l'OM et relance le club en Ligue Europa !

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Buteur décisif avec l'Olympique de Marseille sur la pelouse du Bayer Leverkusen ce jeudi 5 novembre 2026, Amine Gouiri a offert un succès précieux aux Phocéens en Ligue Europa. L'attaquant marseillais n'a d'ailleurs pas caché son immense soulagement au coup de sifflet final.

Après une entame européenne particulièrement délicate marquée par trois défaites consécutives sur la scène continentale cette saison, Marseille a enfin débloqué son compteur lors de la 4e journée de la phase de ligue. Au micro de Canal+, Amine Gouiri a ainsi qualifié cette victoire à la BayArena de « soirée parfaite », tout en soulignant qu'il s'agissait du « match référence en Coupe d'Europe » pour l'OM. Une libération totale pour le groupe. D'ailleurs, l'attaquant olympien a révélé avoir eu « un peu la rage » à la mi-temps après avoir manqué une occasion très nette dès la 7e minute face au portier allemand Mark Flekken.

L'attaquant marseillais a finalement forcé la décision d'une belle frappe à la 77e minute de jeu. Après avoir bénéficié d'une ressortie de balle pour s'engouffrer dans l'espace, le joueur a repiqué dans l'axe avant de décocher sa frappe enroulée devant le parcage marseillais. Amine Gouiri confirme ainsi sa bonne forme actuelle, lui qui totalise désormais 3 buts et 2 passes décisives sur ses quatre derniers matches toutes compétitions confondues avec le club phocéen. Une efficacité redoutable. En conférence de presse, Bruno Genesio a salué le talent et la confiance grandissante de son joueur tout en appelant à tempérer l'euphorie pour conserver la régularité.

Grâce à cette victoire étriquée mais capitale sur la pelouse du Bayer Leverkusen (0-1), l'OM remonte à la 31e place du classement de la Ligue Europa avec 3 points au compteur. Les Olympiens ne comptent plus qu'une seule unité de retard sur le Sturm Graz, actuel 24e et virtuel barragiste. Selon Opta, l'Olympique de Marseille possède désormais 60,4 % de chances d'accéder au top 24 de la phase de ligue en C3. Rien n'est encore acquis pour la qualification, mais l'espoir renaît pleinement. De quoi conforter l'optimisme de Bruno Genesio à l'issue de la rencontre.

Toutefois, les joueurs olympiens n'auront pas le loisir de savourer longtemps cette victoire acquise en Allemagne. Le calendrier national impose en effet un retour immédiat aux affaires courantes, avec une affiche particulièrement attendue dans le nord du pays. Un test d'envergure en perspective. Le prochain rendez-vous de l'OM est fixé au dimanche 8 novembre 2026 à 20h45 sur la pelouse du RC Lens au stade Bollaert-Delelis pour le compte de la 10e journée de Ligue 1.

After gobbling up EA, Saudi Arabia and private equity firm Silver Lake reportedly looking to buy Unity

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

After acquiring EA for $55bn earlier this year, Saudi Arabia and private equity firm Silver Lake are reportedly in the market for another massive video game company. And according to new reports, it's Unity.

Per Reuters, Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund (PIF) and Silver Lake - two major actors in the EA acquisition - are in talks to acquire the video game engine company for around $25bn. Silver Lake's Egon Durban already sits on the Unity Board of Directors, and the firm owns around eight percent of its shares already thanks to a $400m investment.

This $25bn valuation should be taken with a grain of salt for two core reasons. For one, it was decided prior to Unity's latest quarterly financial report, which included a 59% increase in strategic revenue year-over-year and net income of $122m. So this could propel the valuation upwards.

On the other hand, wider economic changes could have a detrimental effect. Reuters points out: "Strong ⁠growth in Unity's AI-powered Vector advertising platform helped it post its first quarterly profit on a GAAP basis since going public in September 2020." Given we've seen the generative AI sector take a major hit this week following the US midterm elections, it's possible companies using the tech may be viewed with more fiscal skepticism. Or, maybe this dip is just temporary. In short, take the $25bn valuation as a ballpark figure.

Unity has had something of an interesting past. There was of course the banana's runtime fee scandal of 2024 in which the company attempted to charge developers a fee on each game sale using the engine, which proved a PR nightmare and was quickly walked back. Unity also recently partnered with Google for AI-game creation platforms Google PlayGround and Unity Spark. Spark is shown to have created a Space Corridor game on its website that looks dubious at best - not exactly a resounding testament to the future.

The last time EA and Silver Lake bought something gaming related it was EA, a Western giant that would go on to lay off hundreds of staff and close BioWare months later. It is worth emphasising that the deal was a leveraged buyout, which saddles EA with substantial debt, whereas the exact nature of this proposed Unity deal is not confirmed. As such if it does happen, it may be a good ol' fashioned private acquisition, not this terms and conditions-laden deal that EA, in its newest guises, must contend with. We've already seen BioWare closed down as a result of this acquisition.

Expect more news about the Unity buyout when official information comes to light.

7 Exercises to Help You Survive a Multi-Day Ski Trip, According to a Physical Therapist

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Autumn belongs to apple orchards and slow cookers, unless you’re a skier. Then it belongs to gear hunts, fresh wax, and lift ticket research. The excitement lasts from the first turning leaf to about 10 P.M. on opening day. Then day two arrives. Your quads burn, your calves feel tight, and you wonder why you skipped those hip and groin stretches.

To understand why day two hits so hard, I spoke to Milica McDowell, DPT, owner of Clearwater Physical Therapy in Bozeman, Montana. She has 20-plus years of clinical experience, grew up skiing at Alyeska Resort in Alaska, and is married to Paul Herberger, a retired Freeride World Tour skier turned coach. So she knows a thing or two about the day-two body aches.

On day one, McDowell says, most skiers feel “absolutely nothing…except for adrenaline.” On day two, the tables turn and “most of us feel like we’ve been hit by a snowcat overnight.” She links the inevitable discomfort to three causes.

The first is eccentric loading, which happens when a muscle works while it lengthens, like the lowering half of a squat. In one study, 24 fit skiers spent four hours on the slopes. A day later, they still had reduced eccentric strength in their hamstrings and quads. McDowell calls the result “itty-bitty microtraumas in our muscle.” A 2019 research review puts soreness from unfamiliar eccentric work at its worst 24 to 72 hours after.

The second is a gap between gym fitness and ski fitness. Strength training and cardio help, McDowell says, but they’re “not specific to the demands of carving turns for hours on end.” A study of elite racers shows skiing leans on slow lowering and long, steady holds. While physical therapist Matt Hastings points to slow-tempo lifts and wall sits, McDowell notes that it’s “really really hard to prepare for hours in that flexed ankle and flexed knee position.”

The third is altitude. Thin air makes it harder to deliver oxygen to your muscles, so fatigue sets in sooner, and Cleveland Clinic says headache and fatigue can show up within a day or so of arriving.

As McDowell puts it, “it’s harder work to do the work.” Some soreness is normal, but it rarely lasts more than five days. If you wake up feeling hungover “without knocking back too many après PBRs,” McDowell says, thin air might be why.

None of that is a reason to skip day two. The best time to fix it is before you go. McDowell’s plan starts four to six weeks out, with three strength moves and one cardio drill. Start light, because new eccentric work causes its own soreness.

The last three moves on the list can be used as a five-minute warm-up.

Stand with feet shoulder-width apart. Drop as deep as feels comfortable, then rise without locking out your hips and knees. Pulse for 20 to 30 seconds, building to 60 to 75 seconds, for four to six sets. It can “mimic the demands of repeated turns,” McDowell says.

Pop one heel up and hold the kettlebell on that side. Hinge down with a flat back, then stand tall with glutes fired. Most fit people can use 25 pounds, McDowell says. Do 12 to 20 reps for two to four sets.

It’s “just a fancy lunge,” McDowell says, with your back foot on a bench or step and a weight in one hand. Herberger suggested this one. Do eight to 12 reps for two to five sets.

McDowell likes short bursts of hard effort, 30 to 60 seconds long, followed by three to four minutes of rest. Aim for your heart rate to climb toward about 75 percent of your max, which the American Heart Association estimates at about 220 minus your age. If you have heart, kidney, or metabolic disease, talk to your doctor first. If you’re new to exercise, start lower and build.

McDowell’s warm-up starts with stairs, squats, or burpees until your heart rate climbs 20 to 30 beats per minute above resting. Then run through three moves for your calves, quads, and hips.

It copies your ski boot position and gives you “a nice stretch into your Achilles area.”

If you’re sore on day two, three, or four, lie on your side. Pull your heel to your seat and take slow breaths. A review of 99 studies found stretching didn’t reduce soreness, so think comfort, not cure. The same review found light movement and massage eased soreness, so take a walk between ski days.

A lunge with a twist toward the ceiling. It’s “a fantastic way to warm up your spine, rotators, core, and lower body all in one.” Herberger suggested this one too.

Day two sends the bill whether you plan for it or not. Joe Puchek, 73, has skied for 71 years, and he told SKI to “make the decisions today to allow you to ski tomorrow.” The wax can wait. The squats can’t.

Want more Outside health stories? Sign up for the Bodywork newsletter. And for tips on how to train, fuel, and recover better, sign up for the Sweat Science 101 newsletter.
